{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01X9ak2F4LijwVFcKvFgKx5e","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# OpenPowerlifting Data Distribution README\n     2→For a rendered version of this document, [view the README on GitLab](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/docs/data-readme.md).\n     3→\n     4→\n     5→### Name\n     6→Mandatory. The name of the lifter in UTF-8 encoding.\n     7→\n     8→Lifters who share the same name are distinguished by use of a `#` symbol followed by a unique number. For example, two lifters both named `John Doe` would have `Name` values `John Doe #1` and `John Doe #2` respectively.\n     9→\n    10→### Sex\n    11→Mandatory. The sex category in which the lifter competed, `M`, `F`, or `Mx`.\n    12→\n    13→Mx (pronounced *Muks*) is a gender-neutral title — like Mr and Ms — originating from the UK. It is a catch-all sex category that is particularly appropriate for non-binary lifters.\n    14→\n    15→The `Sex` column is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/sex.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/sex.rs).\n    16→\n    17→### Event\n    18→Mandatory. The type of competition that the lifter entered.\n    19→\n    20→Values are as follows:\n    21→\n    22→- SBD: Squat-Bench-Deadlift, also commonly called \"Full Power\".\n    23→- BD: Bench-Deadlift, also commonly called \"Ironman\" or \"Push-Pull\".\n    24→- SD: Squat-Deadlift, very uncommon.\n    25→- SB: Squat-Bench, very uncommon.\n    26→- S: Squat-only.\n    27→- B: Bench-only.\n    28→- D: Deadlift-only.\n    29→\n    30→\n    31→### Equipment\n    32→Mandatory. The equipment category under which the lifts were performed.\n    33→\n    34→Note that this *does not mean that the lifter was actually wearing that equipment!* For example, GPC-affiliated federations do not have a category that disallows knee wraps. Therefore, all lifters, even if they only wore knee sleeves, nevertheless competed in the `Wraps` equipment category, because they were allowed to wear wraps.\n    35→\n    36→Values are as follows:\n    37→\n    38→- Raw: Bare knees or knee sleeves.\n    39→- Wraps: Knee wraps were allowed.\n    40→- Single-ply: Equipped, single-ply suits.\n    41→- Multi-ply: Equipped, multi-ply suits (includes Double-ply).\n    42→- Unlimited: Equipped, multi-ply suits or rubberized gear (like Bench Daddies).\n    43→- Straps: Allowed straps on the deadlift (used mostly for exhibitions, not real meets).\n    44→\n    45→### Age\n    46→Optional. The age of the lifter on the start date of the meet, if known.\n    47→\n    48→Ages can be one of two types: exact or approximate. Exact ages are given as integer numbers, for example `23`. Approximate ages are given as an integer plus `0.5`, for example `23.5`.\n    49→\n    50→Approximate ages mean that the lifter could be either of *two* possible ages. For an approximate age of `n + 0.5`, the possible ages are `n` or `n+1`. For example, a lifter with the given age `23.5` could be either `23` or `24` -- we don't have enough information to know.\n    51→\n    52→Approximate ages occur because some federations only provide us with birth year information. So another way to think about approximate ages is that `23.5` implies that the lifter turns `24` that year.\n    53→\n    54→\n    55→### AgeClass\n    56→Optional. The age class in which the filter falls, for example `40-45`. These classes are based on exact age of the lifter on the day of competition.\n    57→\n    58→AgeClass is mostly useful because sometimes a federation will report that a lifter competed in the 50-54 divison without providing any further age information. This way, we can still tag them as 50-54, even if the `Age` column is empty.\n    59→\n    60→\n    61→### BirthYearClass\n    62→Optional. The birth year class in which the filter falls, for example `40-49`. The ages in the range are the oldest possible ages for the lifter that year. For example, `40-49` means \"the year the lifter turns 40 through the full year in which the lifter turns 49.\"\n    63→\n    64→`BirthYearClass` is used primarily by the IPF and by IPF affiliates. Non-IPF federations tend to use `AgeClass` instead.\n    65→\n    66→\n    67→### Division\n    68→Optional. Free-form UTF-8 text describing the division of competition, like `Open` or `Juniors 20-23` or `Professional`.\n    69→\n    70→Some federations are *configured* in our database, which means that we have agreed on a limited set of division options for that federation, and we have rewritten their results to only use that set, and tests enforce that. Even still, divisions are not standardized *between* configured federations: it really is free-form text, just to provide context.\n    71→\n    72→\n    73→### BodyweightKg\n    74→Optional. The recorded bodyweight of the lifter at the time of competition, to two decimal places.\n    75→\n    76→### WeightClassKg\n    77→Optional. The weight class in which the lifter competed, to two decimal places.\n    78→\n    79→Weight classes can be specified as a maximum or as a minimum. Maximums are specified by just the number, for example `90` means \"up to (and including) 90kg.\" minimums are specified by a `+` to the right of the number, for example `90+` means \"above (and excluding) 90kg.\"\n    80→\n    81→`WeightClassKg` is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/weightclasskg.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/weightclasskg.rs).\n    82→\n    83→### Best3SquatKg, Best3BenchKg, Best3DeadliftKg\n    84→Optional. Maximum of the first three successful attempts for the lift.\n    85→\n    86→Rarely may be negative: that is used by some federations to report the lowest weight the lifter attempted and failed.\n    87→\n    88→### TotalKg\n    89→Optional. Sum of `Best3SquatKg`, `Best3BenchKg`, and `Best3DeadliftKg`, if all three lifts were a success.\n    90→If one of the lifts was failed, or the lifter was disqualified for some other reason, the `TotalKg` is empty.\n    91→\n    92→Rarely, mostly for older meets, a federation will report the total but not *any* lift information.\n    93→\n    94→### Place\n    95→Mandatory. The recorded place of the lifter in the given division at the end of the meet.\n    96→\n    97→Values are as follows:\n    98→\n    99→- Positive number: the place the lifter came in.\n   100→- G: Guest lifter. The lifter succeeded, but wasn't eligible for awards.\n   101→- DQ: Disqualified. Note that DQ could be for procedural reasons, not just failed attempts.\n   102→- DD: Doping Disqualification. The lifter failed a drug test.\n   103→- NS: No-Show. The lifter did not show up on the meet day.\n   104→\n   105→The `Place` column is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/place.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/place.rs).\n   106→\n   107→\n   108→### Tested\n   109→Optional. `Yes` if the lifter entered a drug-tested category, empty otherwise.\n   110→\n   111→Note that this records whether the results *count as drug-tested*, which does not imply that the lifter actually took a drug test.\n   112→Federations do not report which lifters, if any, were subject to drug testing.\n   113→\n   114→### Country\n   115→Optional. The home country of the lifter, if known.\n   116→\n   117→The full list of valid Country values is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/country.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/country.rs).\n   118→\n   119→### State\n   120→Optional. The home state/province/oblast/division/etc of the lifter, if known.\n   121→\n   122→The full list of valid State values is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/states.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/states.rs).\n   123→Expanded names are given there in comments.\n   124→\n   125→### Federation\n   126→Mandatory. The federation that hosted the meet.\n   127→\n   128→Note that this may be different than the international federation that provided sanction to the meet.\n   129→For example, USPA meets are sanctioned by the IPL, but we record USPA meets as `USPA`.\n   130→\n   131→The full list of valid Federation values is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/federation.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/federation.rs).\n   132→Comments in that file help explain what each federation value means.\n   133→\n   134→### ParentFederation\n   135→Optional. The topmost federation that sanctioned the meet, usually the international body.\n   136→\n   137→For example, the `ParentFederation` for the `USAPL` and `EPA` is `IPF`.\n   138→\n   139→### Date\n   140→Mandatory. The start date of the meet in [ISO 8601 format](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_8601).\n   141→ISO 8601 looks like `YYYY-MM-DD`: as an example, `1996-12-04` would be December 4th, 1996.\n   142→\n   143→Meets that last more than one day only have the start date recorded.\n   144→\n   145→### MeetCountry\n   146→Mandatory. The country in which the meet was held.\n   147→\n   148→The full list of valid Country values is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/country.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/country.rs).\n   149→\n   150→### MeetState\n   151→Optional. The state, province, or region in which the meet was held.\n   152→\n   153→The full list of valid State values is defined by [crates/opltypes/src/state.rs](https://gitlab.com/openpowerlifting/opl-data/blob/main/crates/opltypes/src/state.rs).\n   154→\n   155→### MeetName\n   156→Mandatory. The name of the meet.\n   157→\n   158→The name is defined to never include the year or the federation.\n   159→For example, the meet officially called `2019 USAPL Raw National Championships` would have the MeetName `Raw National Championshps`.\n   160→\n   161→### Sanctioned\n   162→Optional. Whether the meet counts as officially sanctioned by a federation we recognize.\n   163→\n   164→Values are \"Yes\" for sanctioned and \"No\" for unsanctioned.\n   165→\n   166→Defaults to \"Yes\".\n   167→\n   168→Sanctioned meets are competitions that are recognized by a standards body that we recognize (a federation).\n   169→A federation may also sometimes choose to host an unsanctioned meet with a relaxed ruleset for novices.\n   170→\n   171→Unsanctioned meets are competitions that were put on by individual meet directors, typically outside of a federation.\n   172→In OpenPowerlifting, unsanctioned meets are tracked, but do not count for rankings or records.\n   173→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. It provides capabilities to access worksheets, read/write cell values, and work with formulas while maintaining Excel's native format.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install openpyxl\n```\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n### Opening a Workbook\n```python\nfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n\n# Load existing workbook\nwb = load_workbook('/path/to/file.xlsx')\n\n# Access sheets\nsheet_names = wb.sheetnames\ndata_sheet = wb['Data']\ndots_sheet = wb['Dots']\n```\n\n### Reading Data\n```python\n# Get all values from a sheet\nfor row in data_sheet.iter_rows(values_only=True):\n    print(row)\n\n# Get specific cell\ncell_value = data_sheet['A1'].value\n\n# Get column values\ncolumn_a = [cell.value for cell in data_sheet['A']]\n```\n\n### Writing Data\n```python\n# Set cell value\ndata_sheet['A1'] = 'New Value'\ndots_sheet['A1'] = 'Name'\n\n# Add formula to cell\ndots_sheet['B1'] = '=SUM(C1:E1)'\n\n# Write to multiple cells\nfor idx, name in enumerate(names, start=1):\n    dots_sheet[f'A{idx}'] = name\n```\n\n### Copying Data Between Sheets\n```python\n# Copy all rows from one sheet to another\nfor row_idx, row in enumerate(data_sheet.iter_rows(values_only=False), start=1):\n    for col_idx, cell in enumerate(row, start=1):\n        if cell.value is not None:\n            target_sheet.cell(row=row_idx, column=col_idx).value = cell.value\n```\n\n### Working with Columns\n```python\n# Get column header (column names)\nheaders = [cell.value for cell in data_sheet[1]]\n\n# Find column index by name\ndef get_column_index(sheet, header_name):\n    for idx, cell in enumerate(sheet[1], start=1):\n        if cell.value == header_name:\n            return idx\n    return None\n\ncol_index = get_column_index(data_sheet, 'Best3SquatKg')\n```\n\n### Saving Changes\n```python\nwb.save('/path/to/file.xlsx')\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Iterating Through Data Rows\n```python\n# Skip header row\nfor row_idx, row in enumerate(data_sheet.iter_rows(min_row=2, values_only=True), start=2):\n    name, sex, weight = row[0], row[1], row[2]\n```\n\n### Finding Column Positions\n```python\n# Get all column headers\nheaders = {cell.value: idx for idx, cell in enumerate(data_sheet[1], start=1)}\n# Access: headers['Name'] -> column index\n```\n\n### Appending New Columns\n```python\n# Find max column\nmax_col = data_sheet.max_column\n# Add new column header at next position\nnew_col_idx = max_col + 1\ndots_sheet.cell(row=1, column=new_col_idx).value = 'NewColumn'\n\n# Add formulas for each data row\nfor row_idx in range(2, data_sheet.max_row + 1):\n    dots_sheet.cell(row=row_idx, column=new_col_idx).value = f'=FORMULA(A{row_idx})'\n```\n\n## Tips\n- Always use `load_workbook()` before any operations\n- Cell references use Excel notation: 'A1', 'B2', etc.\n- Formulas are stored as strings starting with '='\n- Use `values_only=True` when you only need values, not cell objects\n- Save {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01KHFYDtbdhhkTHxFjC7JV2M","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01G1buV3cFoL6NW5Xriqr7do","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/dots-powerlifting-formula.md","content":"# DOTS Powerlifting Scoring Formula\n\n## Overview\nDOTS (Dynamic Observational Training System) is a powerlifting scoring coefficient that normalizes total weight lifted across different body weights and genders. It was adopted by the International Powerlifting Federation (IPF) in 2019.\n\n## Formula Structure\n```\nDOTS Score = Total (kg) × DOTS Coefficient\n\nDOTS Coefficient = 500 / Denominator\n\nDenominator = a×BW⁴ + b×BW³ + c×BW² + d×BW + e\n```\n\nWhere:\n- BW = Body weight in kilograms\n- a, b, c, d, e = Gender-specific polynomial coefficients\n\n## Coefficients\n\n### Male Coefficients\n- a = -0.0000010930\n- b = 0.0007391293\n- c = -0.1918759221\n- d = 24.0900756\n- e = -307.75076\n\n### Female Coefficients\nFemale coefficients differ to account for physiological differences in strength-to-weight ratios. Based on standard DOTS implementations:\n- a = -0.0000010930\n- b = 0.0007391293\n- c = -0.1918759221\n- d = 14.6865503\n- e = -137.27013\n\n(Note: Some sources use different female coefficients. Verify with your federation's specific documentation.)\n\n## Required Data for Calculation\n\nTo calculate DOTS, you need:\n1. **Sex**: M or F (determines which coefficients to use)\n2. **BodyweightKg**: Athlete's body weight at competition\n3. **Best3SquatKg**: Best squat lift in kg\n4. **Best3BenchKg**: Best bench press lift in kg\n5. **Best3DeadliftKg**: Best deadlift lift in kg\n\n## Calculation Steps\n\n1. **Calculate Total**: Sum the three lifts\n   ```\n   Total = Best3SquatKg + Best3BenchKg + Best3DeadliftKg\n   ```\n\n2. **Select Coefficients**: Choose male or female coefficients based on Sex value\n\n3. **Calculate Denominator**: Apply polynomial with body weight\n   ```\n   Denominator = a×BW⁴ + b×BW³ + c×BW² + d×BW + e\n   ```\n\n4. **Calculate DOTS Coefficient**:\n   ```\n   DOTS Coefficient = 500 / Denominator\n   ```\n\n5. **Calculate DOTS Score**:\n   ```\n   DOTS = Total × DOTS Coefficient\n   ```\n\n## Excel Formula Implementation\n\n### Total Calculation (with null handling)\n```excel\n=IF(AND(NOT(ISBLANK(L2)),NOT(ISBLANK(M2)),NOT(ISBLANK(N2))),L2+M2+N2,\"\")\n```\n\n### DOTS Calculation (with gender-specific coefficients)\n```excel\n=IF(NOT(ISBLANK(O2)),\n  500/(IF(B2=\"M\",\n    -0.0000010930*I2^4+0.0007391293*I2^3-0.1918759221*I2^2+24.0900756*I2-307.75076,\n    -0.0000010930*I2^4+0.0007391293*I2^3-0.1918759221*I2^2+14.6865503*I2-137.27013\n  ))*O2,\n  \"\")\n```\n\n## Precision\n- Keep calculations to 3 decimal places: 123.456\n- Use ROUND function if needed: =ROUND(value, 3)\n\n## Sources\n- IPF Formula Documentation: https://www.powerlifting.sport/\n- OpenPowerlifting: https://github.com/sstangl/openpowerlifting\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1068,"cache_read_input_tokens":27194,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1068,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"45960ef4-4843-4449-8377-d9965685bcf5","uuid":"cb3eef26-b2da-4f01-a0aa-578e29d621a0"}

It was adopted by the International Powerlifting Federation (IPF) in 2019.\n\n## Formula Structure\n```\nDOTS Score = Total (kg) × DOTS Coefficient\n\nDOTS Coefficient = 500 / Denominator\n\nDenominator = a×BW⁴ + b×BW³ + c×BW² + d×BW + e\n```\n\nWhere:\n- BW = Body weight in kilograms\n- a, b, c, d, e = Gender-specific polynomial coefficients\n\n## Coefficients\n\n### Male Coefficients\n- a = -0.0000010930\n- b = 0.0007391293\n- c = -0.1918759221\n- d = 24.0900756\n- e = -307.75076\n\n### Female Coefficients\nFemale coefficients differ to account for physiological differences in strength-to-weight ratios. Based on standard DOTS implementations:\n- a = -0.0000010930\n- b = 0.0007391293\n- c = -0.1918759221\n- d = 14.6865503\n- e = -137.27013\n\n(Note: Some sources use different female coefficients. Verify with your federation's specific documentation.)\n\n## Required Data for Calculation\n\nTo calculate DOTS, you need:\n1. **Sex**: M or F (determines which coefficients to use)\n2. **BodyweightKg**: Athlete's body weight at competition\n3. **Best3SquatKg**: Best squat lift in kg\n4. **Best3BenchKg**: Best bench press lift in kg\n5. **Best3DeadliftKg**: Best deadlift lift in kg\n\n## Calculation Steps\n\n1. **Calculate Total**: Sum the three lifts\n   ```\n   Total = Best3SquatKg + Best3BenchKg + Best3DeadliftKg\n   ```\n\n2. **Select Coefficients**: Choose male or female coefficients based on Sex value\n\n3. **Calculate Denominator**: Apply polynomial with body weight\n   ```\n   Denominator = a×BW⁴ + b×BW³ + c×BW² + d×BW + e\n   ```\n\n4. **Calculate DOTS Coefficient**:\n   ```\n   DOTS Coefficient = 500 / Denominator\n   ```\n\n5. **Calculate DOTS Score**:\n   ```\n   DOTS = Total × DOTS Coefficient\n   ```\n\n## Excel Formula Implementation\n\n### Total Calculation (with null handling)\n```excel\n=IF(AND(NOT(ISBLANK(L2)),NOT(ISBLANK(M2)),NOT(ISBLANK(N2))),L2+M2+N2,\"\")\n```\n\n### DOTS Calculation (with gender-specific coefficients)\n```excel\n=IF(NOT(ISBLANK(O2)),\n  500/(IF(B2=\"M\",\n    -0.0000010930*I2^4+0.0007391293*I2^3-0.1918759221*I2^2+24.0900756*I2-307.75076,\n    -0.0000010930*I2^4+0.0007391293*I2^3-0.1918759221*I2^2+14.6865503*I2-137.27013\n  ))*O2,\n  \"\")\n```\n\n## Precision\n- Keep calculations to 3 decimal places: 123.456\n- Use ROUND function if needed: =ROUND(value, 3)\n\n## Sources\n- IPF Formula Documentation: https://www.powerlifting.sport/\n- OpenPowerlifting: https://github.com/sstangl/openpowerlifting\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01U1kwcDs6VzAV2fJJtA7WME","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01T7T6tG38etiKweNkSWACqX","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/python-xlsx-processing.md","content":"# Python XLSX Processing: Data Analysis and Transformation\n\n## Overview\nProcessing Excel files with Python using OpenPyXL for reading/writing and basic data manipulation without external data science libraries.\n\n## Workflow Pattern\n\n### 1. Load and Inspect\n```python\nfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n\nwb = load_workbook('file.xlsx')\nws = wb['Sheet1']\n\n# Get dimensions\nprint(f\"Rows: {ws.max_row}, Columns: {ws.max_column}\")\n\n# Get headers\nheaders = [cell.value for cell in ws[1]]\nprint(\"Headers:\", headers)\n```\n\n### 2. Extract Column Information\n```python\n# Get all values from a column\ndef get_column_values(ws, column_letter):\n    return [cell.value for cell in ws[column_letter]][1:]  # Skip header\n\n# Get column index by name\ndef find_column_index(ws, column_name):\n    for idx, cell in enumerate(ws[1], start=1):\n        if cell.value == column_name:\n            return idx\n    raise ValueError(f\"Column '{column_name}' not found\")\n```\n\n### 3. Copy Specific Columns to New Sheet\n```python\n# Map source columns to copy\ncolumns_to_copy = ['Name', 'Sex', 'BodyweightKg', 'Best3SquatKg',\n                   'Best3BenchKg', 'Best3DeadliftKg']\n\n# Get column indices\ncol_indices = {col: find_column_index(source_ws, col)\n               for col in columns_to_copy}\n\n# Copy header row\nfor col_num, col_name in enumerate(columns_to_copy, start=1):\n    target_ws.cell(row=1, column=col_num).value = col_name\n\n# Copy data rows\nfor row_idx in range(2, source_ws.max_row + 1):\n    for col_num, (col_name, col_idx) in enumerate(col_indices.items(), start=1):\n        source_cell = source_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=col_idx)\n        target_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=col_num).value = source_cell.value\n```\n\n### 4. Add Calculated Columns with Formulas\n```python\n# Add new column header\nnext_col = target_ws.max_column + 1\ntarget_ws.cell(row=1, column=next_col).value = 'TotalKg'\n\n# Add formula for each row\nfor row_idx in range(2, target_ws.max_row + 1):\n    # Formula references must use current sheet columns\n    # Assuming Squat is col 4, Bench col 5, Deadlift col 6\n    formula = f'=IF(AND(NOT(ISBLANK(D{row_idx})),NOT(ISBLANK(E{row_idx})),NOT(ISBLANK(F{row_idx}))),D{row_idx}+E{row_idx}+F{row_idx},\"'\n    target_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=next_col).value = formula\n```\n\n### 5. Handle Data Validation\n```python\n# Check for missing required values\ndef validate_row(ws, row_idx, required_columns):\n    for col_idx in required_columns:\n        if ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=col_idx).value is None:\n            return False\n    return True\n\n# Get column indices for required fields\nrequired_cols = [find_column_index(ws, 'Sex'),\n                find_column_index(ws, 'BodyweightKg'),\n                find_column_index(ws, 'Best3SquatKg'),\n                find_column_index(ws, 'Best3BenchKg'),\n                find_column_index(ws, 'Best3DeadliftKg')]\n```\n\n## Key Excel Formula Patterns\n\n### Conditional Aggregation\n```excel\n=IF(AND(NOT(ISBLANK(D2)),NOT(ISBLANK(E2)),NOT(ISBLANK(F2))),D2+E2+F2,\"\")\n```\nPrevents calculation if any component is missing.\n\n### Conditional DOTS (Gender-based)\n```excel\n=IF(NOT(ISBLANK(TotalCol)),\n  500/(IF(SexCol=\"M\",male_formula,female_formula))*TotalCol,\n  \"\")\n```\n\n## Complete Example: DOTS Processing\n```python\nfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n\nwb = load_workbook('powerlifting.xlsx')\ndata_ws = wb['Data']\ndots_ws = wb['Dots']\n\n# Headers to copy\nheaders = ['Name', 'Sex', 'BodyweightKg', 'Best3SquatKg',\n           'Best3BenchKg', 'Best3DeadliftKg']\n\n# Find column indices in source\ncol_map = {h: None for h in headers}\nfor col_idx, cell in enumerate(data_ws[1], start=1):\n    if cell.value in col_map:\n        col_map[cell.value] = col_idx\n\n# Copy headers and data\nfor new_col, header in enumerate(headers, start=1):\n    dots_ws.cell(row=1, column=new_col).value = header\n\nfor row_idx in range(2, data_ws.max_row + 1):\n    for new_col, header in enumerate(headers, start=1):\n        source_col = col_map[header]\n        dots_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=new_col).value = \\\n            data_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=source_col).value\n\n# Add TotalKg column\ntotal_col = len(headers) + 1\ndots_ws.cell(row=1, column=total_col).value = 'TotalKg'\nfor row_idx in range(2, data_ws.max_row + 1):\n    sq_col, b_col, d_col = 4, 5, 6\n    dots_ws.cell(row=row_idx, column=total_col).value = \\\n        f'=IF(AND(NOT(ISBLANK(D{row_idx})),NOT(ISBLANK(E{row_idx})),NOT(ISBLANK(F{row_idx}))),D{row_idx}+E{row_idx}+F{row_idx},\"\")'\n\n# Save\nwb.save('powerlifting.xlsx')\n```\n\n## Tips\n- Always verify column indices match between sheets\n- Use string concatenation for dynamic formula generation\n- Test formulas with sample data first\n- Save workbook after all modifications\n- Handle empty cells
